Q.5 Find a real number $\alpha$ so that the
vectors $\underline{u}$ and $\underline{v}$ are perpendicular.
(i) $\underline{u}=\alpha\underline{i}+3\underline{j}+\underline{k}, \underline{v}=\underline{i}-2\underline{j}+\alpha\underline{k}$
The vectors $\underline{u}$ and $\underline{v}$ are perpendicular
So $\underline{u}.\underline{v}=0$
$(\alpha\underline{i}+3\underline{j}+\underline{k}).(\underline{i}-2\underline{j}+\alpha\underline{k})=0$
$\alpha-6+\alpha=0 \Rightarrow 2\alpha-6=0$
$\alpha=3$
(ii) $\underline{u}=\alpha\underline{i}+2\alpha\underline{j}-\underline{k}, \underline{v}=\underline{i}+\alpha\underline{j}+3\underline{k}$
The vectors $\underline{u}$ and $\underline{v}$ are perpendicular
So $\underline{u}.\underline{v}=0$
$(\alpha\underline{i}+2\alpha\underline{j}-\underline{k}).(\underline{i}+\alpha\underline{j}+3\underline{k})=0$
$\alpha+2\alpha^2-3=0 \Rightarrow 2\alpha^2+\alpha-3=0$
$2\alpha^2+3\alpha-2\alpha-3=0$
$\alpha(2\alpha+3)-1(2\alpha+3)=0$
$(\alpha-1)(2\alpha+3)=0$
Either $\alpha-1=0$ or $2\alpha+3=0$
$\alpha=1$ or $\alpha=\frac{-3}{2}$

Q.6 Find the number $z$ so that the
triangle with vertices
$A(3,0,-2), B(0,3,1)$ and $C(1,1,z)$ is
right triangle with right angle at $C$.
$\vec{AC}=(\underline{i}+\underline{j}+z\underline{k})-(3\underline{i}+0\underline{j}-2\underline{k})$
$=-2\underline{i}+\underline{j}+(z+2)\underline{k}$
$\vec{BC}=(\underline{i}+\underline{j}+z\underline{k})-(0\underline{i}+3\underline{j}+\underline{k})$
$=\underline{i}-2\underline{j}+(z-1)\underline{k}$
As $\vec{AC}.\vec{BC}=0$
$(-2\underline{i}+\underline{j}+(z+2)\underline{k}).(\underline{i}-2\underline{j}+(z-1)\underline{k})=0$
$-2-2+(z+2)(z-1)=0$
$-4+z^2+z-2=0$
$z^2+z-6=0$
$z^2+3z-2z-6=0$
$z(z+3)-2(z+3)=0$
$(z-2)(z+3)=0$
$z=2, z=-3$

Q.7 If $\hat{a}$ and $\hat{b}$ are unit vectors and $2\theta$
is the angle between them, show that
$\sin\theta=\frac{1}{2}|\hat{a}-\hat{b}|$
Since $\hat{a}$ and $\hat{b}$ are unit vectors, so we
must have $|\hat{a}|=1, |\hat{b}|=1$
Considering $|\hat{a}-\hat{b}|^2=|\hat{a}|^2+|\hat{b}|^2-2\hat{a}.\hat{b}$
$\Rightarrow |\hat{a}-\hat{b}|^2=(1)^2+(1)^2-2|\hat{a}|.|\hat{b}|\cos 2\theta$
since angle between given unit vectors
is $2\theta$
Now, simplifying the above expression,
we get
$|\hat{a}-\hat{b}|^2=2-2(1)(1)\cos 2\theta$
$\Rightarrow |\hat{a}-\hat{b}|^2=2-2\cos 2\theta$
$\Rightarrow |\hat{a}-\hat{b}|^2=2(1-\cos 2\theta)$
Using the trigonometric identity
$1-\cos 2\theta=2\sin^2\theta$ we have

--- Page 314 ---
|WANT TO GET ALL SUBJECT NOTES: WWW.FREEILM.COM| |WEBSITE: WWW.FREEILM.COM|

$|\hat{a}-\hat{b}|^2=2(2\sin^2\theta)$
$\Rightarrow|\hat{a}-\hat{b}|^2=4\sin^2\theta\Rightarrow4\sin^2\theta=|\hat{a}-\hat{b}|^2$
$\Rightarrow\sin^2\theta=\frac{1}{4}|\hat{a}-\hat{b}|^2$
Taking square root on both sides, we get
$\sin\theta=\frac{1}{2}|\hat{a}-\hat{b}|$
Hence the proof.
Q.8 If $|\underline{a}+\underline{b}|=|\underline{a}-\underline{b}|$ then show that $\underline{a}$
and $\underline{b}$ are perpendicular.
Given that $|\underline{a}+\underline{b}|=|\underline{a}-\underline{b}|$. squaring
both sides, we get,
$|\underline{a}+\underline{b}|^2=|\underline{a}-\underline{b}|^2$
$\Rightarrow(\underline{a}+\underline{b}).(\underline{a}+\underline{b})=(\underline{a}-\underline{b}).(\underline{a}-\underline{b})$
$\Rightarrow\underline{a}.(\underline{a}+\underline{b})+\underline{b}.(\underline{a}+\underline{b})=\underline{a}.(\underline{a}-\underline{b})-\underline{b}.(\underline{a}-\underline{b})$
$\Rightarrow\underline{a}.\underline{a}+\underline{a}.\underline{b}+\underline{b}.\underline{a}+\underline{b}.\underline{b}=\underline{a}.\underline{a}-\underline{a}.\underline{b}-\underline{b}.\underline{a}+\underline{b}.\underline{b}$
As we know that
$\underline{a}.\underline{b}=\underline{b}.\underline{a}$ and $\underline{a}.\underline{a}=|\underline{a}|^2$ similarly
$\underline{b}.\underline{b}=|\underline{b}|^2$ so we have
$|\underline{a}|^2+2\underline{a}.\underline{b}+|\underline{b}|^2=|\underline{a}|^2-2\underline{a}.\underline{b}+|\underline{b}|^2$
$\Rightarrow2\underline{a}.\underline{b}=-2\underline{a}.\underline{b}\Rightarrow4\underline{a}.\underline{b}=0$
$\Rightarrow\underline{a}.\underline{b}=0$
Since dot product of both vectors is 0,
therefore we must have $\underline{a}\perp\underline{b}$ which
completes the proof.
Q.9
(i) Show that the vectors $3\underline{i}-2\underline{j}+\underline{k}$,

$\underline{u}.\underline{w}=0\Rightarrow\underline{u}\perp\underline{w}$
So $\underline{u},\underline{v}$ and $\underline{w}$ form a right triangle

$\vec{PQ}.\vec{QR}=(-3\underline{i}+4\underline{j}-3\underline{k}).(-3\underline{i}+\underline{j}+7\underline{k})$
$+9+4-21=13-21=-7\neq0$
So the points do not form a right
triangle.
Q.10 Prove that
$\cos(\alpha+\beta)=\cos\alpha\cos\beta-\sin\alpha\sin\beta$
Let $\vec{OA}$ and $\vec{OB}$ are unit vectors in
$xy$-plane making angles $\alpha$ and $-\beta$
with the
positive $x$-axis respectively.
so that $\angle AOB=\alpha+\beta$
then $\vec{OA}=\cos\alpha\underline{i}+\sin\alpha\underline{j}$
and $\vec{OB}=\cos\beta\underline{i}-\sin\beta\underline{j}$
$\vec{OB}.\vec{OA}=(\cos\beta-\sin\beta).(\cos\alpha+\sin\alpha)$
$|\vec{OA}||\vec{OB}|\cos(\alpha+\beta)=\cos\alpha\cos\beta$
$-\sin\alpha\sin\beta$
$\cos(\alpha+\beta)=\cos\alpha\cos\beta-\sin\alpha\sin\beta$

Q.11 Prove that in any triangle ABC.
(i) b = c cos A + a cos C
Let the vector a, b and c are along the
sides BC, CA and AB of the triangle
ABC
a + b + c = 0
b = -(a + c)
Now taking dot
product with b
b.b = -(a + c).b
b² = -(a.b + c.b) = -a.b - c.b
= -abcos(π - C) - bc cos(π - A)
= -ab(-cos C) - bc(-cos A)
b² = ab cos C + bc cos A
b = a cos C + c cos A
(ii) c = a cos B + b cos A
Let the vectors a, b and c are along
the sides BC, CA and AB of the
triangle ABC
a + b + c = 0 ⇒ c = -a - b
Now taking dot product with c
c.c = -(a + b).c
c² = -(a.c + b.c) = -a.c - b.c
= -ac cos(π - B) - bc cos(π - A)
= -ac cos(π - B) - bc cos(π - A)
= -ac(-cos B) - bc(-cos A)
c² = ac cos B + bc cos A
c = a cos B + b cos A
(iii) b² = c² + a² - 2ca cos B
Let the vectors a, b and c along the
Sides BC, CA and AB of triangle ABC
⇒ a + b + c = 0 ⇒ b = -(a + c)
b.b = -(a + c).b
b² = -(a + c).(-(a + c)) = (a + c).(a + c)
= a.a + a.c + c.a + c.c = a² + 2a.c + c²
= a² + c² + 2c.a
= a² + c² + 2ca cos(π - B)
b² = a² + c² - 2ca cos B

(iv) c² = a² + b² - 2ab cos C
Let a, b and c are vectors along sides
BC, CA and AB of triangle ABC
a + b + c = 0
c = -a - b
c = -(a + b)
c.c = (-(a + b)).(-(a + b))
c² = (a + b).(a + b)
= a.a + a.b + b.a + b.b = a² + 2a.b + b²
= a² + 2a.b + b²
= a² + b² + 2ab cos(π - C)
c² = a² + b² - 2ab cos C
Q.12 Show that for any vectors a and b.
|a| - |b| ≤ |a + b| ≤ |a| + |b|
We know from the previous step that
|x + y| ≤ |x| + |y|
Let x = a + b and y = -b
Then |a| = |(a + b) + (-b)| ≤ |a + b| + |-b|
Since |-b| = |b|
|a| ≤ |a + b| + |b|
Rearrange the inequality:
|a| - |b| ≤ |a + b|
Similarly, Let x = a + b and y = -a
Then |b| = |(a + b) + (-a)| ≤ |a + b| + |-a|
Since |-a| = |a|
|b| ≤ |a + b| + |a|
Rearrange the inequality
|b| - |a| ≤ |a + b|
We have two inequalities:
|a| - |b| ≤ |a + b|
-(|a| - |b|) ≤ |a + b|
These two inequalities together imply:
|a| - |b| ≤ |a + b|

--- Page 316 ---
Q.13 Find the work done, if the point at
which the constant force
F = 2i + 5j + 3k is applied to an
object, moves it from P₁(2,-3,1) to
P₂(7,5,3)
d = P₁P₂ = (7i + 5j + 3k) - (2i - 3j + k)
= 5i + 8j + 2k
Work done = F.d
= (2i + 5j + 3k)•(5i + 8j + 2k)
= 10 + 40 + 6 = 56 units.
Q.14 A particle, acted by constant forces
F₁ = 3i + 4j - 3k and F₂ = i + 4j - k
is displaced from A(2,1,3) to
B(5,4,4) Find the work done.
Here
d = AB = (5-2)i + (4-1)j + (4-3)k
d = 3i + 3j + k
The net force will be F = F₁ + F₂
F = 4i + 8j - 4k
So work done = F.d
= (4i + 8j - 4k)•(3i + 3j + k)
= 12 + 24 - 4 = 32 units
Q.15 A particle is displaced from the point
A(5,-5,-7) to the point B(6,2,-2)
under the action of constant forces
defined by 10i - j + 11k, 4i + 5j + 9k
and -2i + j - 9k Show that the total
work done by the force is 102 units.
Here
d = AB = (6-5)i + (2+5)j + (-2+7)k
d = i + 7j + 5k

And net force is
F = F₁ + F₂ + F₃
F = 12i + 5j + 11k
So work done =
F.d = (12i + 5j + 11k)•(i + 7j + 5k)
= (12)(1) + (5)(7) + (11)(5)
= 12 + 35 + 55 = 102 units
Q.16 A force of magnitude 6 acting parallel
to 4i + 3j - k displaces the point of
application from A(2,-1,3) to
B(7,3,2) Find the work done.
Let the given force be F = 4i + 3j - k
then |F| = √(4)² + (3)² + (-1)² = √26
So required force will be F' = 6F̂
F' = 6(4i + 3j - k / √26)
F' = 24/√26 i + 18/√26 j - 6/√26 k
And displacement is
d = AB = (7-2)i + (3+1)j + (2-3)k
d = 5i + 4j - k
So work done will be
F.d = (24/√26 i + 18/√26 j - 6/√26 k)•(5i + 4j - k)
= 120/√26 + 72/√26 + 6/√26
= 198/√26 units

Q.1 Compute the cross product $\underline{a} \times \underline{b}$ and
$\underline{b} \times \underline{a}$. Check your answer by showing
that each $\underline{a}$ and $\underline{b}$ are perpendicular
to $\underline{a} \times \underline{b}$ and $\underline{b} \times \underline{a}$.
(i) $\underline{a} = 2\underline{i} + \underline{j} - \underline{k}, \underline{b} = \underline{i} - \underline{j} + \underline{k}$
$\underline{a} = 2\underline{i} + \underline{j} - \underline{k}, \underline{b} = \underline{i} - \underline{j} + \underline{k}$
$\underline{a} \times \underline{b} = \begin{vmatrix} \underline{i} & \underline{j} & \underline{k} \\ 2 & 1 & -1 \\ 1 & -1 & 1 \end{vmatrix}$
$= \underline{i}(1-1) - \underline{j}(2+1) + \underline{k}(-2-1)$
$= -3\underline{j} - 3\underline{k}$
$\underline{a}.(\underline{a} \times \underline{b}) = (2\underline{i} + \underline{j} - \underline{k}).(-3\underline{j} - 3\underline{k})$
$= 0 - 3 + 3 = 0 \Rightarrow \underline{a} \perp \underline{a} \times \underline{b}$
$\underline{b}.(\underline{a} \times \underline{b}) = (\underline{i} - \underline{j} + \underline{k}).(-3\underline{j} - 3\underline{k})$
$= (1)(0) + (-1)(-3) + (1)(-3)$
$= 0 + 3 - 3 = 0$
$\Rightarrow \underline{b} \perp \underline{a} \times \underline{b}$
Thus $\underline{a} \times \underline{b}$ is perpendicular to both
vectors $\underline{a}$ and $\underline{b}$
$\underline{b} \times \underline{a} = \begin{vmatrix} \underline{i} & \underline{j} & \underline{k} \\ 1 & -1 & 1 \\ 2 & 1 & -1 \end{vmatrix}$
$= \underline{i}(1-1) - \underline{j}(-1-2) + \underline{k}(1+2)$
$= 3\underline{j} + 3\underline{k}$
$\underline{a}.(\underline{b} \times \underline{a}) = (2\underline{i} + \underline{j} - \underline{k}).(3\underline{j} + 3\underline{k})$
$= 2(0) + (1)(3) + (-1)(3)$
$= 3 - 3 = 0 \Rightarrow \underline{a} \perp \underline{b} \times \underline{a}$
$\underline{b}.(\underline{b} \times \underline{a}) = (\underline{i} - \underline{j} + \underline{k}).(3\underline{j} + 3\underline{k})$
$= (1)(0) + (-1)(3) + (1)(3) = 0 - 3 + 3 = 0$
Thus $\underline{b} \times \underline{a}$ is perpendicular to both
the vectors $\underline{a}$ and $\underline{b}$ .
(ii) $\underline{a} = \underline{i} + 3\underline{j} + 2\underline{k}, \underline{b} = 2\underline{i} - \underline{j} + \underline{k}$

Q.6 Which vectors, if any, are
perpendicular or parallel.
(i) $\underline{u}=5\underline{i}-\underline{j}+\underline{k}; \underline{v}=\underline{j}-5\underline{k}$,

$\underline{u}$ and $\underline{w}$ are parallel

$=(1)(-1)+2(1)-(1)(1)=-1+2-1=0$
The vectors $\underline{u}$ and $\underline{v}$ are perpendicular
$\underline{v}.\underline{w}=(-\underline{i}+\underline{j}+\underline{k}).(-\frac{\pi}{2}\underline{i}-\pi\underline{j}+\frac{\pi}{2}\underline{k})$
$=\frac{\pi}{2}-\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}=0$
The vectors $\underline{v}$ and $\underline{w}$ are perpendicular

Q.7 Use definition of cross product, for any vectors u, v, w and scalar k, prove that
(i) u × (-u) = 0
Let u = u₁i + u₂j + u₃k then -u = -u₁i - u₂j - u₃k . Taking cross product, we have
u × (-u) = | i j k | = i(-u₂u₃ + u₂u₃) - j(-u₁u₃ + u₁u₃) + k(-u₁u₂ + u₁u₂)
           | u₁ u₂ u₃ |
           | -u₁ -u₂ -u₃ |
= 0i + 0j + 0k = 0
(ii) u × v = -v × u
Let u = u₁i + u₂j + u₃k and v = v₁i + v₂j + v₃k then
u × v = | i j k | = i(u₂v₃ - u₃v₂) - j(u₁v₃ - u₃v₁) + k(u₁v₂ - u₂v₁) ……(1)
        | u₁ u₂ u₃ |
        | v₁ v₂ v₃ |
v × u = | i j k | = i(u₃v₂ - u₂v₃) - j(u₃v₁ - u₁v₃) + k(u₂v₁ - u₁v₂) ……(2)
        | v₁ v₂ v₃ |
        | u₁ u₂ u₃ |
Equation (2) can be written as
-v × u = - | i j k | = i(u₂v₃ - u₃v₂) - j(u₁v₃ - u₃v₁) + k(u₁v₂ - u₂v₁) ……(3)
           | v₁ v₂ v₃ |
           | u₁ u₂ u₃ |
From equations (1) and (3), we can conclude that u × v = -v × u
(iii) u × (kv) = (ku) × v = k(u × v)
Since k is a scalar, so it can't be the part of cross product since cross product can only be done
when both are vectors. So it's clear by definition of cross product that
u × (kv) = (ku) × v = k(u × v)

--- Page 322 ---
(iv) u×(v+w)=(u×v)+(u×w)
Using distributive property of cross product, we can deduce the above result.
Q.8 Prove that a+b+c=0
a×(b+c)+b×(c+a)+c×(a+b)=0 Taking cross product with a
Solution: a×(a+b+c)=0⇒a×a+a×b+a×c=0
L.H.S = a×a+a×b+a×c=0
a×(b+c)+b×(c+a)+c×(a+b) 0+a×b-(c×a)=0, a×b=c×a ...(i)
=a×b+a×c+b×c+b×a+c×a+c×b Taking cross product with b
=a×b+a×c+b×c+(-a×b)+(-a×c)+(-b×c) b×(a+b+c)=0, b×a+b×b+b×c=0
=a×b+a×c+b×c-a×b-a×c-b×c -a×b+0+b×c=0, b×c=a×b ...(ii)
= 0 = R.H.S From (i) and (ii), a×b=b×c=c×a

Q.10 Prove that sin(α-β)=sinαcosβ-cosαsinβ

x-axis respectively.
So that m∠BOA=α-β

i j k

cosα sinα 0
|OB||OA|sin(α-β)k=i(0-0)-j(0-0)+k(cosβsinα-sinβcosα) |OA||OB|=1
sin(α-β)k=(sinαcosβ-cosαsinβ)k⇒sin(α-β)=(sinαcosβ-cosαsinβ)
Q. 11 Show that |a×b|²=|a|²|b|²-(a.b)² Q.12 Use definition of cross product, prove
Solution: that for any vectors u and v
We know that |a×b|=|a||b|sinθ where (u+v)×(u-v)=-2(u×v)
θ is the angle between the given
vectors. Solution:
LHS = |a×b|² Here LHS = (u+v)×(u-v)
=(|a||b|sinθ)²=|a|²|b|²sin²θ =u×(u-v)+v×(u-v)
=|a|²|b|²(1-cos²θ) (∵sin²θ=1-cos²θ) =u×u-u×v+v×u-v×v
=|a|²|b|²-|a|²|b|²cos²θ (Distributive property of cross product)
=|a|²|b|²-(|a||b|cosθ)² 0-u×v-u×v-0 since u×u=0 and
=|a|²|b|²-(a.b)² (∵a.b=|a||b|cosθ) v×u=-u×v
= RHS ⇒-2(u×v)=RHS

Q.13 Find the moment about the point
$M(1,-3,3)$ of the force represented
by $\overline{AB}$ where the coordinates of
points $A(4,3,-1)$ and $B(-1,3,7)$ are
given.
Here
$\overline{AB} = (-1-4)\underline{i} + (3-3)\underline{j} + (7+1)\underline{k}$
$\overline{AB} = -5\underline{i} + 8\underline{k}$
And given that
$\underline{r} = \overline{MA} = \overline{OA} - \overline{OM} = (4-1)\underline{i} + (3+3)\underline{j} + (-1-3)\underline{k}$
$\underline{r} = 3\underline{i} + 6\underline{j} - 4\underline{k}$
So moment of the force is given by
$\underline{M} = \underline{r} \times \underline{F} = \begin{vmatrix} \underline{i} & \underline{j} & \underline{k} \\ 3 & 6 & -4 \\ -5 & 0 & 8 \end{vmatrix}$
$\underline{M} = \underline{i}(48+0) - \underline{j}(24-20) + \underline{k}(0+30)$
$\underline{M} = 48\underline{i} - 4\underline{j} + 30\underline{k}$

the point $A(1,-1,2)$. Find the moment
of the force about the point $B(3,-2,3)$.
Here $\overline{F} = 6\underline{i} + 4\underline{j} - 4\underline{k}$ and
$\underline{r} = \overline{BA} = (1-3)\underline{i} + (-1+2)\underline{j} + (2-3)\underline{k}$
$\underline{r} = -2\underline{i} + \underline{j} - \underline{k}$
So moment of the force is given by
$\underline{M} = \underline{r} \times \underline{F} = \begin{vmatrix} \underline{i} & \underline{j} & \underline{k} \\ -2 & 1 & -1 \\ 6 & 4 & -4 \end{vmatrix}$
$\underline{M} = \underline{i}(-4+4) - \underline{j}(8+6) + \underline{k}(-8-6)$
$\underline{M} = -14\underline{j} - 14\underline{k}$

Q.15 Given a force $\underline{F} = 2\underline{i} + \underline{j} - 3\underline{k}$ acting

$B(2,0,-2)$.

$\underline{r} = -\underline{i} - 2\underline{j} + 3\underline{k}$
So moment of the force is given by

$\underline{M} = 15\underline{i} - 15\underline{j} - 15\underline{k}$

Q.6
(a) Find the value of:

= 4(k.k) = 4(1) = 4

(b) Prove that

we know that
u.(v×w) = v.(w×u) = w.(u×v)
= u.(v×w) + u.(v×w) + u.(v×w)
= 3u.(v×w) = R.H.S

Q.8 Prove that the points whose position vectors are A(3i + 2j - k), B(i - 2j + k),
C(6i + 4j - 2k) and D(9i + 6j - 3k) are coplanar.

Q.9 Prove that for any three non-zero vectors u, v and w, (u + v).[(v + w) x (w + u)] = 2[u v w]
Here LHS = (u + v).[(v + w) x (w + u)]
= (u + v).[v x w + v x u + w x w + w x u]
= (u + v).[v x w + v x u + 0 + w x u] (∵ w x w = 0)
= (u + v).[(v x w) + (v x u) + (w x u)]
= [(u + v).(v x w) + (u + v).(v x u) + (u + v).(w x u)]
= [u.(v x w) + v.(v x w) + u.(v x u) + v.(v x u) + u.(w x u) + v.(w x u)]
As we know that a.(a x b) = 0 so we have
v.(v x w) = 0, u.(v x u) = 0, v.(v x u) = 0, u.(w x u) = 0
Hence we are left with only u.(w x u) + v.(w x u)
= u.(w x u) + u.(v x w) ∵ v.(w x u) = u.(v x w)
= 2[u v w] = RHS

Q.10 Consider a parallelepiped determined by the vectors $\underline{u}=2\underline{i}+4\underline{j}-3\underline{k}$, $\underline{v}=5\underline{i}-3\underline{j}+6\underline{k}$ and
$\underline{w}=4\underline{i}-7\underline{j}-2\underline{k}$ If the base of the parallelepiped is defined by the vectors $\underline{u}$ and $\underline{v}$ then find
the height of the parallelepiped.
As we know that the volume of parallelepiped is given by $(\underline{u} \times \underline{v}) \cdot \underline{w}=|\underline{u} \times \underline{v}||\underline{w}| \cos \theta$
$(\underline{u} \times \underline{v}) \cdot \underline{w}=\left|\begin{array}{ccc}2 & 4 & -3 \\ 5 & -3 & 6 \\ 4 & -7 & -2\end{array}\right|$
$(\underline{u} \times \underline{v}) \cdot \underline{w}=2(6+42)-4(-10-24)-3(-35+12)=96+136+69$
$(\underline{u} \times \underline{v}) \cdot \underline{w}=301$
And $\underline{u} \times \underline{v}=\left|\begin{array}{ccc}\underline{i} & \underline{j} & \underline{k} \\ 2 & 4 & -3 \\ 5 & -3 & 6\end{array}\right|$
$\underline{u} \times \underline{v}=\underline{i}(24-9)-\underline{j}(12+15)+\underline{k}(-6-20)$
$\underline{u} \times \underline{v}=15\underline{i}-27\underline{j}-26\underline{k} \Rightarrow|\underline{u} \times \underline{v}|=\sqrt{(15)^{2}+(-27)^{2}+(-26)^{2}}=\sqrt{255+729+676}$
$\Rightarrow|\underline{u} \times \underline{v}|=\sqrt{1630}$
So height of parallelepiped (say $h$) can be computed as $h=|\underline{w}| \cos \theta=\frac{(\underline{u} \times \underline{v}) \cdot \underline{w}}{|\underline{u} \times \underline{v}|}$
$h=\frac{301}{\sqrt{1630}}$ units
Q.11 A mechanic applies a force of 50 pounds along the positive $x$-axis on a wrench connected to a
bolt. The pivot point of the wrench is at the origin $(0,0,0)$ and the force is applied at the point
$(0 \mathrm{ft}, 2 \mathrm{ft}, 3 \mathrm{ft})$ Determine the torque produced by this force about the pivot point.
Here $\underline{F}=50\underline{i}+0\underline{j}+0\underline{k}$ and $\underline{r}=0\underline{i}+2\underline{j}+3\underline{k}$
And we know that torque can be computed as $\underline{\tau}=\underline{r} \times \underline{F}$
$\underline{\tau}=\left|\begin{array}{ccc}\underline{i} & \underline{j} & \underline{k} \\ 0 & 2 & 3 \\ 50 & 0 & 0\end{array}\right|$
$\underline{\tau}=\underline{i}(0-0)-\underline{j}(0-150)+\underline{k}(0-100)$
$\underline{\tau}=150\underline{j}-100\underline{k}$ pound feet

Q.13 The vector u = 50i + 75j + 65k shows how many belts, pants and shirts were sold at a store.
The vector w = 1500i + 3500j + 3000k shows the price (in rupees) of each item. Find u.w and
explain what the result tells us in real life.
Here u.w = (50i + 75j + 65k).(1500i + 3500j + 3000k)
u.w = (50)(1500) + (75)(3500) + (65)(3000)
u.w = 75000 + 262500 + 195000
u.w = 532500
This result shows the total revenue from the sales of all items in rupees.

Q.15 An electric shop sells three types appliances: Fans, Heaters and Ovens. The monthly sales
quantities are 500 units of Fans, 300 units of Heaters and 200 units of Ovens. The profit per
unit for each appliance is Rs.500 for Fans, Rs.400 for Heaters and Rs.2,000 for Ovens.
(a) Represent the monthly sales quantities and the profit per unit as vectors.
(b) Calculate the total monthly profit using vector operations.
(a) Vectors of monthly sales quantities are u = 500i + 300j + 200k or [500, 300, 200] and
v = 500i + 400j + 2000k or [500, 400, 2000] respectively.
(b) Total monthly profit is the dot product of vectors u and v
i.e; u.v = (500i + 300j + 200k).(500i + 400j + 2000k)
u.v = (500)(500) + (300)(400) + (200)(2000)
u.v = 250000 + 120000 + 400000 = 770000
So monthly profit is Rs.770,000
